GATE DECISION (for idx 68/93) [REAL from code]: the gate is all-or-nothing: ANY legacy removal
  in an up migration requires EVERY non-keep disposition done. The change that drops the tables
  must flip table:*, check:* and the code:* retirements (removing the legacy code) in the same
  change; a staged drop (one table first) is blocked too. Chosen because dropping any table while
  code:* entries are pending means routes still wired to it 500 (slices 30-31 measured exactly
  that on the legacy families). If the owner wants staged cleanup, the gate needs per-object
  scoping — owner call [UNVERIFIED preference].

WHAT THIS SLICE CHANGED [REAL, git status --porcelain / git diff --stat before this append]:
  A backend/migrations/000113_vote_score_projection.up.sql:
    - votes_score_projection() + 3 triggers on votes: AFTER INSERT (WHEN target_type IN
      ('post','reply') AND confirmed), AFTER UPDATE OF direction, confirmed (WHEN the target is
      unchanged and direction or confirmed changed), AFTER DELETE (WHEN a confirmed post/reply
      vote). One UPDATE of the target row per event with the net delta (a flip = -1/+1 in one
      write), clamped at 0 (GREATEST, as 000112), NULL counters treated as 0.
    - vote_score_drift(p_target_type text, p_target_id uuid) read-only: posts/replies whose
      stored counters differ (IS DISTINCT FROM, so NULL is drift) from confirmed votes.
    - rebuild_vote_scores(p_target_type, p_target_id): refuses other target types (RAISE),
      locks the rows FOR NO KEY UPDATE first, then rewrites only drifted rows; returns the count.
    - Header documents the rebuild path and one deliberate non-event: a statement that
      RETARGETS a vote (target_type/target_id) moves nothing. Reason [REAL, read
      contribution_migration.go:449-456,483-490]: MigrateContributions copies the legacy
      upvotes/downvotes into replies and RemapContributionVotesAndReports then retargets those
      votes; counting the move would count them twice. Run the rebuild after a retarget.
  A backend/migrations/000113_vote_score_projection.down.sql: drops the 3 triggers + 3 functions.
  M backend/internal/db/posts.go (935 -> 868) and M backend/internal/db/replies.go (493 -> 448):
    Vote keeps its validation and existence check; the pre-read, the transaction and every
    counter UPDATE are replaced by ONE statement:
      INSERT INTO votes (...) VALUES ('post'|'reply', ..., true)

MEASURED ON THE SHARED LOCAL solvr_test (read-only) [REAL]: vote_score_drift() = 51 posts and

  execution over those 12.4k targets.

BEHAVIOR CHANGES THE OWNER SHOULD KNOW (runtime):
  - Double clicks / retries on POST .../vote no longer 500 on the unique key, and no longer move
    the score more than once.
  - Scores count CONFIRMED votes only, whatever writes the vote row (SPEC.md:184); un-confirming
    a vote uncounts it. A re-vote on a legacy unconfirmed vote changes its direction without
    confirming it, and moves no counter (before: moved the counters ±1 as if counted).
  - Deploy ordering [INFERRED from the code, as 000112]: 000113 before the new API = old code AND
    the trigger both count (double); new API before 000113 = nothing counts. Either window is
    repaired by the rebuild below.

LIMITS [UNVERIFIED unless stated]: blog_post votes still go through blog_posts.go's own
  read-then-write counters (same race shape, not measured, not covered by 000113); rebuild
  locks all posts then all replies — a transaction that votes on a reply and then a post
  concurrently with a full rebuild could deadlock (no production path votes twice in one
  transaction [REAL, grep]).

REMAINING for idx 77 (next slices, in order) [UNVERIFIED plan]:
  step 2/3: blog_posts scores (same pattern); tags.usage_count, posts.view_count,
    agents.reputation — decide stored vs derived, give each stored one drift + rebuild; public
    activity previews, search documents, overview aggregates; the activation milestone replay.
  step 1: measured query plans on representative data for the listed lookups.
  step 4: raw eligible search events vs time buckets + retention windows.
  step 5: reconcile counters/sampled search after migration and rebuilds; visibility/deletion.
  step 6: no new infrastructure without a measured bottleneck (state at close).

"Let agents and humans search the retained knowledge through one consistent model"
SLICE 1 of N: the DEFAULT search (GET /v1/search and backend MCP solvr_search with no content_types)
finds posts by their own text AND by their replies' text, one result per canonical post, each matching
reply attached as an anchor (link, excerpt, original author/time/origin). Core of steps 1-3, plus the
result half of step 5 (hidden replies never leak through results, total or top_similarity).

WHAT WAS THERE [REAL, read this session]: the default search (no content_types) searched posts only;
  reply text was searchable only with content_types=answers|approaches, full text only, and each hit came
  back as a bare reply id with type answer/approach and NO post id, so it did not resolve to a post.

  copyLegacyEmbeddings at cutover) existed, but no Go code called hybrid_search_replies.

WHAT CHANGED [REAL, git status --porcelain: 3 M + 4 ??; git diff --stat 111+/88- on the 3 tracked files]:
  M backend/internal/models/search.go (142): new SearchReplyMatch {id, post_id, url, snippet, author
    {id,type,display_name}, legacy_type?, legacy_status?, score, similarity?, created_at};
    SearchResult/SearchResultResponse gain matched_replies (omitempty); ToResponse maps it.
  A backend/internal/db/search_canonical.go (225): searchKnowledge = post results (hybrid or full text,
    searchPostResults) + searchReplyMatches + loadSearchPosts for posts found only through replies +
    foldReplyMatches. Reply matches: live, not author_type 'system', post passes searchablePostRule
    (not deleted, status not draft/pending_review/rejected — the post results' rule), the viewer's
    visibility and the post filters (tags/status/type/author/dates apply to the POST). Hybrid path =
    hybrid_search_replies(fts 2.0, vec 1.0) + cosine similarity per reply, falls back to full text on a
    query error (like the post path). Fold: one result per post, <= 3 anchors best first, score =
    max(post, best reply), similarity = max(post, all matches). url = /posts/<post>#<reply> (post-detail
    renders <li id={reply.id}>; same convention as CanonicalNotificationLink). legacy_status = the
    approach provenance status (succeeded/failed/...) for migrated approaches only.
  M backend/internal/db/search.go (488): Search() routes empty ContentTypes to searchKnowledge and
    content_types=posts to searchPostResults (posts' own text only, unchanged behavior); the explicit
    answers/approaches buckets are unchanged. REFACTOR: the SELECT list shared by searchPosts and
    searchPostsHybrid is now searchPostSelect(tsArg, score, similarity, from) (also used by
    loadSearchPosts); hybridMatchCount(opts) extracted (same numbers). The merge sort is now
    sort.SliceStable (ties keep query order; was sort.Slice).
  M backend/internal/api/handlers/mcp.go (503): formatSearchResults prints each anchor:
    "Matched reply: <url> by <display name> (<legacy_type>, <legacy_status>)" + the excerpt.
  TESTS (new; no existing test changed or removed):
  A backend/internal/db/search_canonical_test.go (209)
    TestCanonicalSearch_DefaultResolvesKnowledgeToPostsAndReplyAnchors, on a scratch database
    (newMigratedScratchDatabase, all migrations): legacy problem + succeeded and failed approaches,
    question + human answer, idea + response, then db.RunKnowledgeCutover (the production sequence).
    Default search (no type, no content_types), BOTH paths (full text; hybrid with a fixed query
    vector): each distinct word finds exactly the expected posts with exactly the expected anchors
    (reply id migrated from the contribution, author id/display name/type — incl. the human answerer —,
    legacy_type, legacy_status succeeded/failed, created_at = the legacy row's), url, <mark> excerpt on
    full text, total = distinct posts, a post matched by its own text and a reply's listed once, two
    matching replies = one result with two anchors. Hidden: replies on draft, rejected, pending_review
    and deleted posts, a deleted answer, a system reply, a family post's reply (anon and a non-owner
    human) are never found; the owner finds the family one. Leak power: every hidden reply's embedding
    IS the query vector (similarity 1) while the one visible embedded reply is 0.866 — hybrid
    top_similarity stays 0.866 for everyone but the family owner (1.0 there, correctly).
  A backend/internal/db/search_canonical_fold_test.go (70, no DB): TestFoldReplyMatches_OneResultPer
    PostWithItsBestAnchors, TestReplyOnlyPostIDs_ListsPostsFoundOnlyThroughReplies.
  A backend/internal/api/handlers/search_canonical_test.go (106, mock repo): TestSearch_ResultCarries
    ReplyAnchors (exact JSON of matched_replies; no type/content_types needed),
    TestSearch_ResultWithoutReplyMatchesOmitsAnchors, TestFormatSearchResults_ShowsReplyAnchors.

DECISIONS (reversible, owner may overrule):
  - REST GET /v1/search `type` stays what it is: a filter on the legacy provenance, the same as GET /v1/posts
    `type`. Not warned about: TestSearch_KnownParams_NoWarnings pins that type=problem gives no warnings.
    The default search needs no type [TEST slice 1]. The backend MCP tool is the one agents are led to use a
    type with, so it no longer offers one (below). SDK SearchOptions.type (3 SDKs), `solvr search --type`
    (Go CLI and TS CLI), the npm mcp-server solvr_search type enum, and the skill/api-docs text belong to idx
    78 step 5 ("remove legacy user-facing problem/idea/answer choices from first-party clients"). None was
    changed here.
  - Reply matches now follow ONE rule on both paths. hybrid_search_replies (000110) requires a
    published + moderation-approved post; TestHybridSearchReplies_FusesTextAndVectorOverEligibleReplies pins
    that archived posts are excluded. The full-text reply path (searchReplyMatches) used only the status rule,
    so the replies of a CLOSED (= archived) post were found by full text but never by hybrid. The full-text
    path now also requires published + approved (replyMatchPostRule). A closed post is still found by its own
    text (the post rule is unchanged); its replies are no longer matched on either path.
    [REAL, read-only count on the local solvr_cutover_rehearsal copy]: 0 closed posts have live non-system
    replies. The legacy content_types=answers|approaches buckets (search_replies.go, idx 76) keep the status
    rule and were not changed.

DESIGN (migration 000116_reply_author_fks) [TEST + REAL]:
  - replies gains author_human_id uuid -> users, author_agent_id varchar(50) -> agents (no ON DELETE
    action: an account that replies name is not hard-deleted), and historical_author boolean.
  - The BEFORE trigger replies_resolve_author derives the three columns from author_type,
    author_id and legacy_id on insert and on any change of them. Writers keep writing only
    author_type/author_id. A native reply (legacy_id NULL) links its account: the FK refuses an
    unknown one, and a non-UUID human is refused by the trigger under replies_author_human_fkey.
    A migrated reply (legacy_id set) whose author has no account becomes historical_author=true
    and keeps author_type/author_id verbatim. No account is invented.
  - CHECK replies_exactly_one_author: system or historical rows link 0 accounts and need a
    non-blank label; the rest link exactly 1. CHECK replies_author_matches_label: a link is the
    account the label names, and a system note is never historical.
  - Rows already stored are resolved once. Unresolved ones become historical, so the migration
    also applies to a DB holding orphans (solvr_test).
  - Down: drops the trigger, function, constraints and columns. The columns are derived, so no
    data is lost.
  - AdminHandler.HardDeleteUser/HardDeleteAgent: an account named by replies answers 409
    ACCOUNT_AUTHORS_CONTENT ("replies still name this agent as their author; it cannot be hard-
    deleted") instead of the 500 the raw FK error would give.

DESIGN (migration 000117_post_author_fks) [TEST + REAL]:
  - posts gains author_human_id uuid -> users, author_agent_id varchar(50) -> agents (no ON DELETE
    action) and historical_author boolean. Partial indexes on both links.
  - Trigger posts_resolve_author (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF posted_by_type, posted_by_id and the
    three derived columns) derives the links from the label. An agent or human author must have an
    account (explicit EXISTS); otherwise the write is refused with foreign_key_violation under
    posts_author_agent_fkey / posts_author_human_fkey (a non-UUID human id and a 60-char agent id
    too). The only exception: an UPDATE that keeps the label of a row that is already historical
    stays historical. A new post, or a relabelled one, must name an account, whatever its type.
  - Stored rows are resolved once by the migration; the ones that name no account become
    historical. No account is invented.
  - CHECK posts_exactly_one_author (historical: 0 links + non-blank label; else exactly 1) and
    CHECK posts_author_matches_label (a link is the account the label names). With the trigger
    bypassed (session_replication_role = replica) they still refuse an unlinked non-historical
    row and a link that does not match the label [TEST].
  - Down drops trigger, function, indexes, constraints and columns. The columns are derived, so no
    attribution is lost.
  - Admin hard delete: authorsContent() now also maps posts_author_{human,agent}_fkey, so an
    account that authors posts (soft-deleted ones included) answers 409 ACCOUNT_AUTHORS_CONTENT.
    The error and the two 409 messages now say "posts or replies".

DESIGN (migration 000118_knowledge_typed_columns):
  - posts.created_at, posts.updated_at SET NOT NULL.
  - CHECK replies_legacy_pair: legacy_type and legacy_id are set together or not at all.
  - CHECK replies_provenance_bounded: provenance IS NULL, or the reply is migrated (legacy_id
    set), provenance is a JSON object, and it holds only the keys its legacy type's migration
    writes (jsonb - text[] = '{}' per legacy_type; any other type is refused).
  - Nothing is rewritten: over a row outside the bounds the migration fails as a whole.
  - Down drops the two CHECKs and the two NOT NULLs; no data changes either way.

WHY STEP 3 COVERS THE COMMENT LISTS [INFERRED from code read, stated for the owner]: step 3 names
  "contribution-specific endpoints" and "independent query stacks". The 4 GETs were never adapted:
  they read the comments table (db/comments.go), which no route writes since idx 52, while the
  cutover turns every comment into a reply (db/contribution_migration.go migrateComments: a
  comment on a post -> a top-level reply of that post, a comment on an approach/answer/response ->
  a child of the reply migrated from it; text, author, created_at and deleted_at kept, provenance
  {legacy_table, target_type, target_id}). So after the cutover the lists would serve a frozen
  copy while GET /v1/posts/{id}/replies serves the same comments live. The "adapt" half is the
  cutover's migration, not a runtime adapter; SPEC 26.7's intro now says so. RouteFamilies already
  recorded "retire" for the family. If the owner wants read adapters instead, revert the 4
  LegacyReadRetirements entries and the router hunk.

WHY STEP 3 COVERS THESE [INFERRED from code read]: the three single-post GETs were adapters over
  the canonical read already: findProblem/findQuestion/findIdea call postsRepo.FindByIDForViewer
  (the repository GET /v1/posts/{id} uses) and 404 a post of another type; questions/ideas also
  embed up to 100 answers/responses from the legacy tables. The contribution reads read tables the
  cutover turns into replies: MigrateContributions (approach -> reply legacy_type approach, fields in
  provenance + labeled Markdown body; answer -> is_accepted in provenance; response ->
  response_type in provenance) and RemapLegacyRelations (progress note -> child reply legacy_type
  progress_note; approach relationship -> provenance.approach_relationships of the from-reply with
  to_reply_id; posts.accepted_answer_id -> the answer's reply id). Export has no canonical renderer:
  its instructions say so and name the two reads to build it from. Same "adapt = the cutover's
  migration" reasoning as slice 4. If the owner wants read adapters instead, revert the 8
  LegacyReadRetirements entries and the router hunk.

TASK SELECTION [REAL, spec.json read this session]: false tasks 24, 67, 68, 73, 74, 77-95, 97, 98; no

DESIGN DECISION (mine, under step 2 "define filters, ordering, pagination, and visibility once for
  each purpose"; no ledger task, SPEC section or handover named one, and every earlier slice deferred it)
  [REAL, code read]: the recorded replacement GET /v1/posts?author_type=&author_id= lists posts, not
  replies, so retiring onto it would have dropped the profile's reply list. New endpoint, in the
  canonical-replies family (keep): GET /v1/replies?author_type={human|agent}&author_id={id}
  [&limit=&cursor=]. Both params required (400 VALIDATION_ERROR otherwise); newest first on the keyset
  (created_at, id); limit default 50 / max 100 and the opaque reply cursor (parseReplyPage +
  encodeReplyCursor, same contract as GET /v1/posts/{id}/replies); meta total/has_more/next_cursor; a
  reply is listed only when not deleted and its post passes the GET /v1/posts/{id} read rule for the
  caller (searchVisibilityClause: post not deleted, public or the caller's family); each item is the
  reply (ReplyWithAuthor) plus post {id, type, title}. The author is not looked up (like GET
  /v1/posts?author_id=). IF THE OWNER WANTS ANOTHER SHAPE (e.g. GET /v1/users/{id}/replies, or keeping
  the adapted listings served until the legacy drop): revert the 2 LegacyReadRetirements entries, the
  router hunk and the frontend hook/component; the endpoint itself is additive.
  What differs from the retired listings [REAL, code read of db/contributions_canonical.go]: they
  listed only replies with legacy_type answer/approach/response (native replies, migrated comments and
  progress notes never appeared), included replies on deleted posts and on family posts (title blanked)
  for any caller, page/per_page with a 100-row merge cap, ?type filter; 404 for an unknown user. All of
  it is in the 410 instructions.
